### 2026-09-21 carbohydrate-quality correction — white bread / GI / GL
Status: **NARROW REOPEN OF WHITE-BREAD SLOT; USER CORRECTLY IDENTIFIED A MISSING AUDIT DIMENSION**.

- The prior plan-level closure was too broad: macro/micro/UL calibration was closed, but **glycemic quality and refined-carbohydrate distribution were not explicitly quantified in the final audit**. Earlier notes mentioned GI qualitatively, which is not enough for a whole-system nutrition review.
- Why white bread existed: it was an **assistant-designed functional calorie tool**, not a Ron health preference or a nutritional requirement. Rationale was calorie density, low fiber/bulk in an already ~44-g-fiber diet, easy sandwiching/portioning, cost and portability, with much of it positioned before training. This rationale must now be weighed against glycemic/refined-grain quality rather than treated as automatically optimal.
- Current modeled white-bread quantity is **970 g/week = ~138.6 g/day average**. Using the current UNO base-case label (54.5 g carbohydrate/100 g), that is ~**75.5 g carbohydrate/day**, about **18.6% of the ~406-g/day carbohydrate budget**. On ordinary days the model uses **170 g white bread**; the 120-g afternoon sandwich alone contributes ~**65.4 g carbohydrate** from white bread.
- Published white-bread GI values vary materially (roughly low-60s to mid-70s on the glucose=100 scale in representative studies). Applying that range only as a sensitivity bound gives the 120-g bread portion a **standalone component GL roughly ~41–50** and the average daily white-bread component ~**47–57 GL units**. These are not predictions of Ron's actual mixed-meal glucose curve; chicken/yogurt/fat/vegetables and subsequent activity alter the response.
- Do **not** assume Nimet whole-wheat sourdough is automatically low-GI. The 2021 international GI tables show breads, including whole-grain/wholemeal breads, span low/medium/high GI depending on processing. A 2026 systematic review judged the evidence that sourdough itself improves glycemic/insulinemic response in healthy people insufficient/very-low certainty. Nimet's stronger reasons are whole-grain/fiber and low salt, not a proven branded GI value.
- Long-term evidence nevertheless favors higher whole-grain/fiber carbohydrate quality over relying heavily on refined grains. A 2019 Lancet systematic-review series found strongest chronic-disease evidence for fiber/whole grains; a 2023 prospective-cohort meta-analysis found higher whole-grain intake associated with lower CHD/CVD/all-cause mortality, whereas refined-grain evidence was weaker/less consistent. High dietary GI/GL has also shown adverse associations in large cohort meta-analyses, though these are observational and not a diagnosis for Ron.
- **Assistant proposal, not Ron decision:** keep refined white bread only where it has a clear performance/adherence function (especially a tolerable pre-training carbohydrate slot), and reassess most non-training white-bread grams against Nimet/other whole-grain bread, pasta, bulgur or legumes. Do not simply replace all white bread 1:1 with more wholemeal bread because fiber/volume is already high and wholemeal bread is not guaranteed low-GI.
- Next calculation should be **meal-level carbohydrate quality / approximate GL**, not a fake single exact GI for the whole diet. Compare at least: current plan vs training-targeted white bread + lower-refined rest-day carbs, while preserving calories, protein, fiber tolerance, sodium, prep time, taste and cost.


### 2026-09-21 carbohydrate-quality / GI-GL audit closure
Status: **GI/GL DIMENSION NOW AUDITED; PLAN CHANGE PROPOSED, NOT YET ADOPTED BY RON**.

- The earlier nutrition closure was incomplete because glycemic quality had been treated qualitatively rather than as an explicit decision dimension. This is now corrected.
- GI must not be treated as a food-health score. The 2021 International GI Tables show dairy, legumes, pasta and many fruits are usually low-GI, while breads and even wholemeal/wholegrain breads span low/medium/high GI depending on processing. Mixed-meal response also depends on carbohydrate amount, protein/fat/fiber and activity. Therefore use **approximate meal GL / carbohydrate quality**, not a fake exact GI for Ron's branded mixed meals.
- Current synchronized high-impact carbohydrate structure: white bread **970 g/week**, Nimet whole-wheat sourdough **700 g/week**, dry pasta **640.2 g/week**, oat flour **560 g/week**, Eti Etimek **222 g/week**, pekmez **70 g/week**, plus fruit/lentils/dairy/vegetables.
- Using current label carbohydrate amounts and representative published GI ranges only as a sensitivity model, an ordinary-day carbohydrate-component GL is roughly: breakfast **~40–54** (fine oat flour dominates), 10:00 **~45–55**, lunch **~27–42** (pasta), 16:45 **~54–64** (120 g white bread + banana dominates), dinner **~21–34**. These are **not predicted blood-glucose excursions**; mixed-meal protein/fat/fiber and subsequent activity can materially alter the actual response.
- Whole-day weighted dietary GI is plausibly around the **moderate** range rather than uniformly high (rough sensitivity envelope roughly mid-40s to around 60), while total GL is necessarily high because the diet intentionally contains ~400 g carbohydrate/day at ~3100 kcal. High absolute daily GL in this high-energy context is not by itself evidence of dysglycemia.
- White bread is therefore **not the sole or necessarily largest glycemic-quality issue**. The current 970 g/week contributes ~75.5 g carbohydrate/day on average and, with representative white-bread GI ~62–73, roughly **47–55 GL units/day** as a component. Fine oat flour and the Sunday Etimek load are also material. The current ordinary 16:45 meal is the clearest avoidable concentration of fast carbohydrate.
- Oat processing matters: randomized human work shows flour/finer particles produce a higher postprandial glycemic response than coarser/whole oat particles. The owned İpek oat flour remains a temporary stock bridge, so there is no need to waste it; when depleted, **assistant proposal** is to prefer ordinary rolled/coarser oats rather than fine/instant oat flour if prep/taste remain acceptable.
- Current pasta is a favorable carbohydrate tool for this purpose: the international GI database commonly places spaghetti around **GI 33–52**, and the 2021 tables identify pasta as usually low-GI. Ron's planned pasta is also eaten chilled, and controlled studies suggest cooling/reheating pasta can attenuate glycemic response, although the size of effect and transfer to Ron should not be assumed exactly.
- **Assistant proposal, not Ron decision:** reduce white bread from **970 -> 320 g/week**, retaining **80 g at 16:45 on the four planned training days (Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri)** as the low-fiber convenient carb slot. Replace the other 650 g/week approximately as follows while preserving the meal structure: on each training day replace 50 g white at 10:00 with 50 g extra Nimet and replace 40 g of the 16:45 white bread with 30 g extra dry pasta at lunch; Wednesday replace 80 g white with 50 g extra Nimet + 25 g extra dry pasta; Saturday replace 170 g white with 100 g extra Nimet + 60 g extra dry pasta; Sunday replace 40 g white with 50 g extra Nimet. Resulting weekly controls would be white bread **320 g**, Nimet **~1.10 kg**, dry pasta **~845.2 g**, before any later Etimek change.
- Using current Turkish label/TürKomp macros, this proposed swap changes the daily average by only about **-5.7 kcal, +2.0 g protein, -0.86 g fat, -4.2 g carbs, +3.1 g fiber**. Applied to the current working envelope, that is roughly **3058–3071 kcal / 147.5–148.3 P / ~98.3 F / 402.0–403.4 C / ~46.8 g fiber**. This is actually closer to the protein/carbohydrate targets while materially reducing refined white bread.
- Glycemic effect of the proposal is meaningful but not magical: white-bread component GL falls from roughly **47–55/day average to ~15–18/day**; the extra Nimet+pasta adds some GL back, so whole-day GL likely drops only by roughly **a single-digit to low-teens amount/day** depending the true Nimet GI. The more robust gain is that the ordinary training-day 16:45 carbohydrate load falls by roughly **13–16 GL units** and the refined-grain share is substantially lower.
- Do **not** automatically replace every refined carb with whole-wheat bread. Nimet's exact GI is unknown and wholemeal breads can still be high-GI; pushing all 650 g into Nimet would also raise fiber/volume substantially. The mixed Nimet+pasta replacement is chosen to balance whole-grain quality, lower GI, calorie density, GI tolerance, prep burden and cost.
- **Sunday remains the next glycemic-quality hotspot:** 148 g Eti Etimek is a large refined-wheat load even if the 40 g white-bread slot is removed. Do not pretend the problem disappears by deleting white bread. A future candidate is to replace part of Sunday Etimek with an ambient-safe legume (e.g. unopened ready chickpeas) plus Nimet, but do not change several logistics-critical foods simultaneously before tolerance is tested.
- No evidence here diagnoses insulin resistance, prediabetes or harmful glucose excursions in Ron. If future clinical glucose/HbA1c data, symptoms, or CGM data exist, they override generic GI modeling.


### 2026-09-21 adversarial omission audit — post GI/GL challenge
Status: **NUTRIENT ARCHITECTURE REOPENED NARROWLY FOR MATERIAL GAPS; DO NOT CALL FULLY CLOSED YET**.

- Ron correctly challenged whether additional dimensions besides GI/GL were missed. Cross-check against the active whole-system factor map found one concrete reference-value defect plus several execution-dependent gaps.
- **Concrete defect: manganese risk framing.** Current hybrid model still carries a fallback around **~9.1–9.4 mg Mn/day**. Earlier closure compared this mainly with the US 11-mg UL and called it acceptable. EFSA 2023 could not establish a UL and instead set a conservative **8 mg/day adult safe level**; EFSA explicitly states that intake above this safe level does **not necessarily mean harm**, but the plan cannot be called manganese-closed while the working estimate is above it. The estimate is also donor-sensitive and TürKomp lacks complete Mn coverage. Recalculate source contributions before changing food.
- **Second-order interaction:** the assistant-proposed GI/GL swap (white bread 970 -> 320 g/week, Nimet ~700 -> ~1100 g/week plus more pasta) may increase manganese because whole-grain wheat is a major Mn source. Therefore that GI proposal is **PAUSED / NOT TO BE ADOPTED** until manganese is re-estimated. A lower-refined-carb solution must not solve GI by worsening Mn/fiber burden.
- **Fat-quality gap:** the latest Turkish-product model has not re-closed current saturated fat/trans fat after the dairy/egg/bread/product corrections. Old baseline values are stale. WHO 2026 still emphasizes replacing saturated/trans fats with unsaturated fats; structurally the plan is olive-oil/nut heavy and likely favorable, but current exact %energy should be recalculated before final closure.
- **Free-sugar/dental gap:** current free sugar appears modest (pekmez is now only ~10 g/day and whole fruit/lactose are not free sugar), but final model has not explicitly quantified product-added sugars (especially Etimek/bread) or exposure frequency. WHO recommends <10% energy, ideally <5%, and identifies free sugar as the main dietary caries risk. Keep pekmez/sugary items inside meals rather than repeated grazing; dental/oral exposure is a real factor even if metabolic sugar totals are low.
- **Iodine/sodium interaction remains open:** iodine deficiency is unlikely, but exact Moova/Birşah iodine plus actual iodized-salt use is unknown. Given the Turkish dairy iodine study and EFSA iodine UL 600 µg/day, the open question is not only deficiency but possible high intake if high-iodine dairy is combined with substantial iodized salt. Actual discretionary salt use must be observed before closing sodium/iodine together.
- **Protein-distribution gap:** total protein is clearly adequate, but after reducing yogurt/milk and changing chicken/pasta amounts, per-meal protein/leucine distribution was not re-verified quantitatively. For resistance training, current ISSN guidance uses ~0.25 g/kg high-quality protein or ~20–40 g per feeding as a practical MPS target. This is an optimization issue, not a deficiency diagnosis.
- **Hydration/electrolyte gap:** still execution-dependent. Ron's physical work + resumed strength training + Istanbul heat can materially change fluid/sodium need. Do not force WHO population sodium limits as an athletic prescription without sweat context. One or two same-condition pre/post-training body-mass checks can estimate sweat loss and are higher-value than guessing.
- **Energy target remains a hypothesis:** ~3100 kcal is not validated until ~2 adherent weeks of same-condition weight trend, waist, appetite/recovery and training performance. A perfect nutrient table at the wrong energy level is still the wrong diet.
- **GI/fiber/lactose tolerance remains empirical:** ~44–47 g fiber/day, daily wheat/oats/lentils, 400 ml milk and yogurt can be excellent if tolerated but can cause bloating/fullness/urgency in some people. No paper model can close this without execution.
- **Late-meal/sleep interaction remains empirical:** the schedule can put dinner close to sleep. Meal size/volume/fat/fiber placement should be judged by actual sleep onset, reflux/fullness and recovery, not assumed harmless from nutrient totals.
- **Food-safety lifecycle remains next mandatory phase:** cold pasta/chicken/lentils require rapid cooling, <=4–5°C storage/transport, conservative poultry doneness and 3–4-day refrigerated-leftover limits or freezing later portions. USDA still recommends poultry 165°F/74°C, shallow-container rapid cooling and most leftovers within 3–4 refrigerated days. This is not a nutrient issue but can dominate health outcome.
- **Adherence/taste/time/logistics remain unverified:** actual prep minutes, eating speed, container volume, monotony, smell/mess, work-fridge reliability, taste and fallback-meal frequency can invalidate an elegant model.
- Lower-priority issues reviewed but not currently action-driving: antinutrient/phytate effects (iron/zinc margins and vitamin-C context are strong), mercury (sardines are low-mercury), oxalate/lectin optimization, single-food antioxidant scores, insulin-index chasing, exotic supplements and microbiome-count targets. Reopen only with symptoms, labs or a concrete decision trigger.
- **Optional performance layer not yet optimized:** creatine/caffeine or other ergogenic aids are not required to make the diet adequate; evaluate separately after the food/execution baseline is stable so supplement optimization does not delay launch.


### 2026-09-21 antioxidant / polyphenol sub-audit checkpoint
- Antioxidants are included in the broader food-system audit, but **not as a single ORAC/total-antioxidant score**. The useful targets are actual food pattern, vitamins C/E/carotenoids, polyphenol-rich foods, fat oxidation/overheating exposure, and clinically relevant outcomes.
- Current plan already has strong conventional antioxidant coverage: vitamin C ~156 mg/day and vitamin E ~16.7 mg/day in the Turkish-calibrated model, plus carotenoid-rich parsley/pepper/tomato/carrot and polyphenol sources including grapes, extra-virgin olive oil, walnuts/almonds, flax, oats/whole grains, legumes and grape pekmez. Exact total polyphenol mg is not reliably calculable from product labels and should not be fabricated.
- Recent human systematic-review/meta-analysis evidence supports that polyphenol interventions can influence gut microbiota/SCFAs, including butyrate, but heterogeneity is substantial and microbiome shifts are not automatically equivalent to clinically meaningful benefit. Use diverse polyphenol-rich whole foods rather than chasing isolated antioxidant supplements.
- Do not add high-dose antioxidant supplements merely to increase 'antioxidant capacity'. NIH evidence summaries note that vitamin C supplementation has not consistently prevented CVD/cancer and high-dose vitamin E has not shown general prevention benefit and can carry harms in some populations. Food-first remains the default.
- Remaining antioxidant-quality questions for final audit: plant-color/food diversity across the week; EVOO freshness/storage and heating; nut/seed rancidity/storage; cooking/charring/AGE/acrylamide exposure; tea/coffee timing if used; and whether low-cost seasonal additions improve polyphenol diversity without increasing cost/complexity. These are being evaluated as part of the broader microbiota/polyphenol/oral-health audit.


### 2026-09-21 broad food-system / oral / microbiome / bioactive / contaminant audit
Status: **BROAD MATERIAL-FACTOR SCREEN COMPLETE; QUANTITATIVE CLOSURE STILL OPEN FOR Mn, current SFA/trans, iodine+salt, and execution validation.**

Scope deliberately extends beyond calories/micros/GI to oral health, microbiota, fermentation, polyphenols/antioxidants, food matrix, antinutrients, cooking chemistry, oxidation, contaminants, processing level and storage.

- **Dental caries/free sugar:** using current base-case labels and weekly quantities, free-sugar exposure is roughly **~13.4 g/day** from Sabıroğlu pekmez (~7.2 g/day sugar), current white-bread label sugar (~4.0 g/day at 970 g/week), and Eti Etimek (~2.2 g/day at 222 g/week). Treating all Nimet label sugars as free sugar gives a conservative upper bound ~**15.8 g/day**, only ~**1.7–2.0% of 3100 kcal**, well below WHO's <10% target and <5% conditional target. Main oral-health issue is therefore **frequency/contact time and sticky refined starch**, not total free-sugar load. Keep pekmez/sugary bread exposures inside defined meals/snacks rather than grazing; water rinse is useful; fluoride toothpaste twice daily and interdental cleaning remain higher-value oral controls than further sugar micro-optimization.
- **Dental erosion:** no routine soda/juice/sports-drink layer is in the baseline, so erosion risk from diet is low. Whole fruit/tomato are not comparable to frequent acidic beverages. Yogurt is acidic but calcium-buffered and is not generally considered an erosive food. Avoid prolonged sipping/holding of acidic drinks if added later.
- **Turkish yogurt microbiology:** under Turkish fermented-milk rules, a product sold as yogurt contains live/active specific starter cultures through shelf life (S. thermophilus + L. delbrueckii subsp. bulgaricus; minimum specific microorganism counts apply). Therefore daily plain yogurt is a genuine fermented-food input, though it is **not proof of permanent colonization or a special probiotic effect**. No probiotic supplement is justified for a healthy baseline merely to increase microbiome diversity.
- **Gut microbiota / SCFA support:** current ~44–47 g/day fiber plus oats, lentils, whole wheat, flax, nuts, fruit/vegetables and yogurt provides a strong substrate pattern. Human RCT/meta-analysis evidence supports fiber increasing Bifidobacterium/Lactobacillus and butyrate, while fermented-food interventions can increase diversity and lower inflammatory markers. Exact taxa are not a target; microbiome composition is highly individual and changes in diversity do not automatically equal clinical benefit.
- **Plant diversity:** current pattern contains multiple plant families but is repetitive. The highest-value microbiome/polyphenol improvement is **rotation within the existing fruit/vegetable volume**, not adding more bulk: rotate red/purple cabbage, onion/garlic, apple/pear/citrus, rocket/spinach/purslane, herbs and seasonal peppers/tomatoes as availability/cost allow. Do not chase an arbitrary '30 plants/week' threshold.
- **Polyphenols/antioxidants:** current plan already supplies vitamin C ~156 mg/day, vitamin E ~16.7 mg/day, carotenoid-rich carrot/tomato/pepper/parsley, and polyphenol/lignan sources from grapes, EVOO, walnuts/almonds, flax, oats/whole grains, lentils and grape pekmez. Exact total polyphenol mg/ORAC is not decision-grade and should not be fabricated. Food-first; no antioxidant supplement stack.
- **Whole grain vs refined grain:** human trials show whole-grain diets can increase fecal butyrate vs refined grains even when major microbiome composition changes are inconsistent. This strengthens the reason to reduce unnecessary refined bread/Etimek exposure, but **not** by simply maximizing Nimet because manganese/fiber must be co-optimized.
- **Phytate/antinutrients:** oats, whole wheat, lentils, nuts and flax contain phytate, which can reduce Fe/Zn/Ca bioavailability. Current iron/zinc/calcium/magnesium margins and vitamin-C context are strong, so no deficiency-driven avoidance is justified. Cooking/fermentation reduce phytate. Revisit only if ferritin/iron/zinc status, GI symptoms or another clinical signal creates a real trigger.
- **Oxalate/lectins:** no current high-risk oxalate pattern is apparent. Almonds/parsley and some plants contribute oxalate, but normal calcium intake and absence of a very high-spinach/cocoa/nut pattern keep this low priority. Properly cooked lentils neutralize lectin concern. Do not restrict these foods without a kidney-stone/clinical trigger.
- **Vegetable nitrate:** nitrate from vegetables/greens is not equivalent to processed-meat nitrite exposure; systematic-review evidence supports increased nitric-oxide bioavailability and possible vascular benefit. Do not avoid parsley/leafy greens for nitrate.
- **AGEs / high-heat chemistry:** current core cooking methods (boiling pasta/lentils, stewing/moist-cooking chicken) are favorable and likely much lower-AGE than grilling/frying/charring. Etimek/toasted bread contributes more dry-heat Maillard products. Human RCT meta-analysis suggests low-AGE patterns can improve several metabolic markers, but exact food AGE tables are method-sensitive. Practical rule: prefer moist/medium-heat cooking, avoid repeated hard searing/char.
- **Acrylamide:** browned/toasted grain foods are the relevant sources. EFSA recommends 'lightly brown, don't burn'; darker/longer toasting raises acrylamide. Human epidemiology has not shown a consistent broad cancer association, so this is a low-cost exposure-minimization rule, not a reason to ban bread/Etimek.
- **Olive-oil oxidation:** EVOO is relatively heat-stable because of MUFA + phenolics, but prolonged/repeated heating degrades phenolics and increases oxidation. Current use is compatible with health: use raw/cold where practical, normal sautéing/stewing is acceptable, avoid repeated deep-fry/reuse and smoking the oil.
- **Nut/flax oxidation + mycotoxins:** nuts/grains/spices can carry aflatoxin/other mycotoxin risk when poorly stored. Buy fresh/reputable product, keep dry/cool/airtight, reject mouldy/discoloured/shriveled/rancid items. Ground flax is more oxidation-prone: grind small batches or keep airtight and cool/refrigerated. This is a storage-control issue, not a reason to remove nuts/flax.
- **Fish contaminants:** sardines are in the FDA/EPA low-mercury 'Best Choices' group; current ~250 g/week is not a mercury-driven problem. Older Turkish canned-fish analyses also found low Hg in sardines, though brand/batch contamination can vary. Fish rotation remains useful but not required solely for mercury at this intake.
- **Histamine/scombrotoxin:** mainly a time-temperature abuse problem in susceptible fish. Commercial sealed cans are low operational risk; once opened, refrigerate promptly and do not leave fish warm for prolonged periods. No routine anti-histamine diet is indicated absent symptoms.
- **Pesticide residues:** regulated food-chain exposure is generally low; washing produce under running water is a cheap control and also reduces microbial contamination. No evidence-based need to peel everything or buy organic by default.
- **Microplastics:** current FDA/WHO evidence remains insufficient to show that levels detected in foods create a proven human-health risk. Do not distort the diet or pay large premiums around this uncertain exposure. Avoid unnecessary heating of food in unsuitable plastic as a general food-contact practice.
- **UPF/additives:** current base is predominantly minimally processed, but white packaged bread and Etimek are the clearest processed/UPF-like convenience items. Observational umbrella evidence associates higher UPF exposure with worse outcomes, but causality/component attribution is uncertain. Reduce these where an equally practical alternative exists; do not panic over calcium propionate, sorbate, lecithin or emulsifiers individually without stronger human evidence.
- **Processed/red meat:** essentially absent from the baseline; this is favorable for long-term colorectal/cardiometabolic risk and removes nitrite/heme/processed-meat concerns.
- **Alcohol/sugary drinks:** not part of the baseline; this is favorable for oral, metabolic and cancer risk.
- **Food matrix/resistant starch:** cooled cooked pasta/lentils may provide somewhat more resistant starch and a lower postprandial response than freshly hot refined starch, but the effect is modest and product/preparation-specific. Treat as a bonus, not a target.
- **Allergy/intolerance/FODMAP/gluten/lactose:** no restriction is justified from the menu alone. Wheat/lentils/dairy can create symptoms in susceptible people; actual tolerance owns the decision. Do not infer celiac disease, lactose intolerance or 'leaky gut' from generic mechanisms.
- **Oral/gut microbiome supplements:** no evidence-based reason to add generic probiotic, prebiotic powder, mouth probiotic or antioxidant supplement while the food pattern is already high-fiber/fermented and execution is untested.
- **Highest-value remaining unknowns after this broad screen:** manganese source decomposition and alternative lower-refined-carb swap; current saturated/trans-fat closure; exact iodine + actual salt use; actual hydration/sweat; food-safety/cold-chain execution; actual GI tolerance; actual dental hygiene/fluoride behavior; and 2-week bodyweight/waist/performance response to the calorie target.


### 2026-09-21 closure continuation — Mn source decomposition + fat-quality recheck
Status: **TWO PAPER GAPS MATERIALLY NARROWED; EXECUTION-ONLY GAPS REMAIN.**

- **Manganese source decomposition:** TürKomp currently does not expose a complete usable Mn field across the required Turkish foods, so source decomposition must remain a foreign-composition sensitivity model rather than Turkish exact-product fact. Using USDA/FoodData Central proxies that reproduce the existing Cronometer ~9.37-mg/day fallback reasonably well, the dominant current contributors are approximately:
  - oat flour 560 g/week at ~4.02 mg Mn/100 g -> **~3.22 mg/day**
  - whole-wheat bread proxy 700 g/week at ~2.17 mg/100 g -> **~2.17 mg/day**
  - dry pasta 640.2 g/week at ~0.92 mg/100 g -> **~0.84 mg/day**
  - almonds 240 g/week at ~2.18 mg/100 g -> **~0.75 mg/day**
  - white bread 970 g/week at ~0.54 mg/100 g -> **~0.74 mg/day**
  - walnuts 105 g/week at ~3.41 mg/100 g -> **~0.51 mg/day**
  - cooked lentils 700 g/week at ~0.49 mg/100 g -> **~0.49 mg/day**
  - flax 35 g/week at ~2.48 mg/100 g -> **~0.12 mg/day**
  - these eight alone sum to **~8.85 mg/day**; minor foods explain why the existing generic full-model result is ~9.37 mg/day.
- The Mn issue is therefore **not primarily nuts/flax**. The largest modeled sources are the temporary **80 g/day oat-flour bridge** and the existing **100 g/day Nimet whole-wheat bread**. Exact Turkish Mn for İpek/Nimet is unavailable, so do not treat 9.37 as laboratory truth.
- EFSA's 2023 adult **safe level is 8 mg/day**, not a UL; EFSA explicitly states intake above a safe level does not necessarily indicate harm. Current generic-model ~9–9.5 mg/day is therefore a **caution/optimization signal, not toxicity evidence**.
- The paused GI proposal (white bread 970 -> 320 g/week; Nimet 700 -> 1100 g/week; pasta 640 -> ~845 g/week) would, using the same proxies, raise manganese by roughly **~1.0 mg/day** because extra whole-wheat bread adds much more Mn than the removed white bread. **Keep that proposal paused.**
- High-ROI implication: do not remove nuts/flax merely to lower Mn; they provide vitamin E/ALA/polyphenol value and relatively little of the total Mn at current doses. If Mn requires reduction, first optimize the large grain sources, especially the temporary oat-flour bridge and any proposed additional whole-wheat bread.
- **Fat-quality recheck:** current food quantities plus exact Dardanel/UNO/Etimek labels and standard composition proxies give saturated fat roughly **~22–23 g/day**, about **~6.5–6.8% of ~3070 kcal**. Total fat around ~99 g/day is ~29% of energy. Both sit below WHO population guardrails of <10% energy from saturated fat and <=30% total fat. Main SFA sources are dairy, olive oil (despite being mostly MUFA), eggs, fish, nuts/oats and packaged bread; the pattern remains strongly unsaturated overall.
- Trans-fat exposure appears **well below the WHO <1% energy limit** in this pattern: there is no partially hydrogenated-fat anchor food, and the remaining trans fat is expected mainly from small natural ruminant-food amounts plus trace packaged-food values. Exact current total cannot be proven from all Turkish labels, so retain a qualitative **low** confidence conclusion rather than a fake decimal.
- **Iodized salt calibration improved:** TürKomp's sampled iodized table salt contains ~950.8 µg iodine/100 g (~9.5 µg/g salt) and ~374 mg sodium/g. Thus a modest 1 g/day of that salt would add only ~9.5 µg iodine and ~374 mg sodium. This supports the view that ordinary modest salt use is more important for sodium than for iodine in this menu; however, exact salt brand and actual grams remain UNKNOWN.
- With food sodium ~1.3–1.5 g/day, roughly **~1–1.8 g/day additional table salt** would bring total sodium to around 1.7–2.2 g/day. Do not prescribe a fixed salt dose without heat/sweat data. The iodine risk remains dominated by uncertain Turkish dairy iodine, not by a small amount of table salt.
- **Protein distribution:** exact final meal-by-meal live Cronometer rows are not currently accessible, but the retained meal architecture strongly suggests three substantial protein feedings (breakfast, lunch, 16:45 chicken sandwich) plus a protein-containing dinner. ISSN's practical resistance-training range is ~20–40 g high-quality protein or ~0.25 g/kg per major feeding every ~3–4 h. A material deficiency in distribution is unlikely; the 10:00 fruit/nut/bread snack does not itself need to hit 20–40 g if adjacent main feedings do. Keep this as **likely adequate / exact row verification pending**, not a reason to add protein.


### 2026-09-21 refined-carb redesign — balanced candidate after Mn check
Status: **ASSISTANT PROPOSAL ONLY / NOT ADOPTED BY RON / DO NOT MUTATE CRONOMETER YET**.

- Rejected candidate remains: white bread 970 -> 320 g/week with Nimet ~1100 g/week; this improves bread GI but likely raises Mn by ~1 mg/day and fiber/volume unnecessarily.
- **Best current balanced candidate:** white bread **970 -> 620 g/week**, keep Nimet at **700 g/week**, increase dry plain pasta **640.2 -> ~840 g/week**, and add about **300 g/week fresh grapes or an energy-equivalent seasonal whole-fruit rotation**.
- Exact arithmetic using current UNO label + TürKomp plain dry pasta + TürKomp fresh-grape comparator:
  - remove 350 g/week UNO white bread;
  - add 200 g/week dry pasta;
  - add 300 g/week fresh grapes;
  - daily-average delta ≈ **+0.2 kcal / -0.72 g protein / -0.24 g fat / +0.33 g carbohydrate / +0.78 g fiber** — effectively macro-neutral.
- Applied to the current working macro envelope, this remains roughly **3064–3077 kcal / ~144.8–145.6 P / ~99.0 F / ~406.5–408 C / ~44.5 g fiber**. Protein is still >2 g/kg at the last-known ~68 kg and does not need arithmetic chasing.
- Mn sensitivity: using the same foreign donor proxies as the ~9.37-mg/day fallback, this swap changes Mn by only about **+0.02 mg/day**, effectively neutral; it avoids the ~+1 mg/day problem created by the high-Nimet proposal.
- GI/GL effect: pasta is usually low-GI in the 2021 international tables while bread is highly variable and often higher. The swap removes ~27.3 g/day average carbohydrate from white bread and replaces it with ~21.2 g/day from pasta + ~6.3 g/day from whole grapes. Depending on the true food GIs, average daily GL plausibly falls by roughly **a few to ~10 units**, but the more important effect is flattening the 16:45 peak by reducing white bread from 120 -> ~80 g on training days.
- Practical distribution candidate:
  - Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri: white bread 50 g at 10:00 + 80 g at 16:45 = 130 g/day;
  - Wed: white bread 50 g at 10:00, none at 16:45 because Etimek already occupies the refined-crisp slot;
  - Sat: white bread 50 g at 10:00; chicken/yogurt/tomato at 16:45 can be eaten without bread or the protein can be shifted within the same day if volume/adherence is poor;
  - Sun: no white bread;
  - total = **620 g/week**.
  - spread the extra 200 g dry pasta across the six pasta days: **~+33 g dry/day**, so ~140 g dry pasta per pasta day instead of ~107 g;
  - spread the extra 300 g grapes across six workdays: **~+50 g/day** (180 -> ~230 g), or rotate an energy-equivalent seasonal whole fruit rather than forcing grapes every day.
- Why this is preferred over pushing white bread to 500 g/week now: further reduction can be made macro-neutral, but it raises lunch volume/fruit volume enough that adherence and work-meal ergonomics become the likely limiting factor before any clear additional health benefit. Start with the 620-g candidate if Ron approves, then reduce further only if the real meals remain easy.
- Whole-grain adequacy remains supported by existing Nimet 700 g/week plus oat/flax/legume pattern. The goal is **not zero refined grain**; it is to avoid unnecessary high-GI/refined concentration while preserving calorie density, low bulk around training and adherence.

### 2026-09-21 execution-safety protocol — ready for launch testing
Status: **ASSISTANT OPERATING PROTOCOL / NOT PROOF OF EXECUTION**.

- Keep the two-batch architecture: **Sunday prepares Mon–Wed; Wednesday prepares Thu–Sat**. This keeps cooked chicken/pasta/lentils inside the ordinary 3–4-day refrigerated-leftover window instead of stretching one Sunday batch through Saturday.
- Cooling: portion cooked food into shallow/small containers promptly; refrigerate within **2 hours**, and within **1 hour** when ambient temperature is above ~32°C. Do not leave a large hot pot at room temperature for prolonged cooling.
- Refrigerator target: **<=4°C** where controllable. Freezer portions are the fallback when a serving would otherwise exceed the fridge window.
- Chicken: target **74°C/165°F in the thickest part**. Without a probe thermometer, use small uniform pieces and a conservative fully-cooked method; if doneness is genuinely uncertain, extend cooking/use a safe fallback rather than guessing from color alone.
- Transport: food leaves home already cold, container sits between/adjacent to the two frozen cold packs, commute is minimized, and food goes directly into the work refrigerator. On very hot/delayed/no-fridge days use a shelf-stable/fresh-bought fallback rather than stretching cold-chain assumptions.
- Cold pasta is acceptable when it was cooked safely, cooled promptly and kept refrigerated; do not rely on 'resistant starch' benefits to justify unsafe cooling.
- Open sardine can: eat promptly; refrigerate leftovers rather than carrying an opened can warm.
- Ground flax: grind small batches or keep airtight/cool; nuts and seeds stay dry/cool/airtight and rancid/mouldy pieces are discarded.
- Bread stock: freeze surplus Nimet/UNO portions and thaw only the near-term amount; this reduces mould/waste without a nutritional downside that matters here.
- First execution-week feedback needed to validate the system: actual prep minutes, container volume, whether lunch is physically comfortable, any bloating/fullness/urgency, whether the 16:45 meal feels too heavy/light before training, and whether cold-chain logistics remain reliable.

### 2026-09-21 hydration test protocol
Status: **EXECUTION MEASUREMENT, NOT A FIXED FLUID PRESCRIPTION**.

- Do not prescribe a rigid daily water number from body mass alone. Use thirst/day conditions, urine pattern and a simple one- or two-session sweat-rate check.
- On one representative gym session, use the same gym scale immediately before and after training with similar clothing and note fluid consumed and any urine. Approximate sweat loss = pre-mass - post-mass + drink intake - urine; divide by session hours for L/h.
- Aim not to finish exercise >~2% lighter from fluid loss and do not intentionally gain weight during exercise. This is a performance/safety guardrail, not a demand to replace 100% of sweat minute-by-minute.
- If a session causes large sweat loss or recovery time is short, replace roughly **100–150% of the measured fluid deficit over recovery**, using normal food/salt for electrolytes unless heat/sweat evidence shows a need for more deliberate sodium replacement.


### 2026-09-21 Ron authorization — refined-carb redesign adopted
Status: **RON DELEGATED CHOICE; ASSISTANT-SELECTED CHANGE IS NOW ADOPTED IN THE PLAN OWNER / CRONOMETER WRITE PENDING CONNECTOR ACCESS**.

Ron explicitly authorized the assistant to choose and apply the better option after the GI/GL + manganese + whole-system audit. This does **not** mean Ron personally selected the detailed numbers; provenance remains assistant-selected under Ron's delegation.

**Adopted plan delta from the synchronized baseline:**
- regular white bread: **970 -> 620 g/week**
- Nimet Tuzsuz Ekşi Mayalı Tam Buğday: **stay 700 g/week**; do not increase it merely to replace white bread
- dry plain pasta: **640.2 -> ~840 g/week**
- add **~350 g/week whole seasonal apple/pear**, preferably about two whole pieces per week, replacing the earlier calibration-only +300 g grape idea. Gala apple TürKomp is ~58 kcal/11.9 C/1.91 fiber per 100 g; Santa Maria pear ~54 kcal/10.96 C/3.50 fiber. Apple/pear improves plant/polyphenol diversity and is generally a lower-glycemic whole-fruit replacement without materially changing calories.
- keep oat flour only as the existing temporary owned-stock bridge; after it is exhausted, prefer ordinary rolled/coarser oats if taste/prep are acceptable rather than rebuying fine oat flour by default.
- no change to D3, milk, yogurt, chicken, eggs, sardines, flax, parsley, nuts or Nimet from this authorization.

**White-bread distribution target:**
- Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri: 50 g at the first work snack + 80 g at 16:45 = 130 g/day
- Wed: 50 g at the first work snack, 0 g at 16:45 because Etimek already supplies the refined crisp-carb slot
- Sat: 50 g at the first work snack, 0 g at 16:45
- Sun: 0 g
- total = **620 g/week**.

**Pasta distribution:** six pasta days at approximately **140 g dry/day** (840 g/week; exact division can be 140 g x6). This supersedes the prior ~106.7 g x6 planning row.

**Fruit implementation:** use about **350 g/week total apple/pear** as whole fruit (e.g. ~2 medium fruits) rather than forcing more grapes. Keep fruit whole, not juice. Exact day may vary; place on non-training/rest days or where appetite allows.

**Expected daily-average effect vs the current baseline:** approximately macro-neutral: roughly **-0 to -2 kcal, -~0.9 g protein, ~0 g fat, -~1.4 g carbohydrate, +~1.3 g fiber/day** depending apple/pear choice. Current total therefore remains close to the ~3060-3080 kcal working envelope; no further calorie correction is justified before execution data.

**Health-system effects of the adopted delta:**
- reduces packaged white bread by ~36%;
- lowers average refined-bread carbohydrate exposure by ~27 g/day;
- lowers the 16:45 training-day white-bread portion 120 -> 80 g;
- keeps Mn effectively neutral instead of raising it with more whole-wheat bread;
- modestly lowers sodium and saturated fat from packaged bread;
- adds whole-fruit/polyphenol/plant-diversity exposure;
- preserves low-GI pasta as the main replacement carbohydrate;
- keeps total fiber in the mid-40-g/day range rather than pushing it sharply higher.

Do **not** apply the previously proposed 320-g/week white-bread / 1.1-kg Nimet version.

**Sunday Etimek:** leave unchanged for the first execution cycle. It remains a known refined-carb hotspot, but changing it simultaneously would add another logistics/GI-tolerance variable. Reassess only after the first execution week or if Ron reports the Sunday meal is poor.

**Live-write state:** the exact Cronometer update is authorized by Ron, but no Cronometer connector is currently available in the active tool environment. Do not falsely claim the diary has been changed. When live Cronometer access returns, update only the clean planned model week and read back totals; do not touch actual eaten records, biometrics, training or macro targets.

- The current Calendar's nutrition entries remain projections, not execution evidence. Do not activate or rewrite them without an explicit Google Calendar change authorization naming the intended schedule delta.

**Timing rationale:** the 16:45 pre-training meal is ~2.5–2.75 h before lifting, enough to digest the planned white-bread/chicken/fruit slot; most daily food volume is shifted earlier, keeping the post-training dinner smaller to protect 22:00 sleep. The two-batch prep schedule keeps chicken/pasta/lentils within ordinary refrigerated-leftover windows and avoids a single 6-day refrigerated batch.


### 2026-09-21 operational reconciliation — dishes, cooking, eating, carrying
Status: **ASSISTANT-SELECTED EXECUTION BASELINE UNDER RON'S DELEGATION / NOT YET EXECUTED / LIVE APPS UNCHANGED**.

The older Sep-08 recipes were nutritionally superseded by the Sep-19/21 controls. The following reconciles dishes, prep, transport and eating with the now-adopted quantities: milk 2.8 L/wk, yogurt 1.05 kg/wk, pasta 840 g/wk, cooked chicken 950 g/wk, Nimet 700 g/wk, white bread 620 g/wk, Etimek 222 g/wk, parsley 105 g/wk, flax 35 g/wk, eggs 8/wk, sardines 250 g/wk, apple/pear 350 g/wk.

**Training days Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri**
- 06:00 breakfast: hot oat-flour porridge from ~80 g flour + ~250 ml milk; 1 boiled egg; 10 g pekmez; 5 g ground flax mixed in after cooking. No routine yogurt here.
- 10:00 work snack: grapes ~180 g, almonds 30 g, walnuts 15 g, Nimet 50 g + white bread 50 g.
- 12:00 lunch: cold pasta from **140 g dry** + cooked chicken 100 g + green pepper 100 g + yogurt 50 g + olive oil 20 g + parsley 15 g; lemon/paprika/water to emulsify into sauce.
- 16:45 pre-training: white bread **80 g** + cooked chicken **70 g** + yogurt 50 g + tomato 50 g; banana ~120 g separately. Keep bread dry until eating; mix chicken+yogurt as filling.
- ~21:10 post-training dinner: cooked red lentils 100 g mashed with yogurt 50 g, Nimet 50 g, carrot 100 g + tomato 50 g, olive oil 15 g, remaining milk ~150 ml. Keep this moist and easy to eat; milk can be drunk separately.

**Wednesday fish/prep day**
- 06:00 same breakfast.
- 10:00 grapes 180 g + almonds 30 g + walnuts 15 g + Nimet 50 g + white bread 50 g; one ~175 g apple/pear can be placed here.
- 12:00: 140 g dry-equivalent cold pasta + chicken 100 g + pepper 100 g + yogurt 50 g + olive oil 10 g + parsley 15 g.
- 16:45: Etimek 74 g + yogurt 50 g + tomato 50 g as dip; banana 120 g. No white bread.
- ~20:20 after prep: Dardanel olive-oil sardines 125 g + lentils 100 g + yogurt 50 g + Nimet 50 g + carrot 100 g + tomato 50 g + milk 150 ml. No extra olive oil.

**Saturday non-training workday**
- 06:00 same breakfast.
- 10:00 grapes 180 g + almonds 30 g + walnuts 15 g + Nimet 50 g + white bread 50 g.
- 12:00 same 140-g-dry pasta/chicken lunch as training days.
- 16:45: move the second 50 g Nimet serving here; assemble Nimet 50 g + chicken 70 g + yogurt 50 g + tomato 50 g; banana separately. No white bread here.
- ~20:00 dinner: lentils 100 g + yogurt 50 g + carrot 100 g + tomato 50 g + oil 15 g + milk 150 ml; no bread because the day's second Nimet serving was moved to 16:45.

**Sunday/library**
- ~07:30 breakfast after prep: oat porridge 80 g + milk 250 ml + 2 boiled eggs + pekmez 10 g + flax 5 g + yogurt 50 g.
- 10:00: grapes 180 g + almonds 30 g + walnuts 15 g + Nimet 50 g.
- 12:00 library: keep sardine can sealed until eating; Dardanel 125 g + Etimek ~100 g + whole washed tomato ~100 g. Assemble small bites immediately before eating; do not pre-soak Etimek.
- 16:45: remaining Etimek ~48 g + banana 120 g + extra almonds 30 g; second ~175 g apple/pear can be placed here or eaten at 10:00 depending appetite. No white bread.
- ~20:00 home: lentils 100 g + yogurt 100 g + Nimet 50 g + carrot 100 g + green pepper 100 g + oil 15 g + milk 150 ml + parsley 15 g.
- Weekly almond total remains 240 g because Sunday has 60 g; walnuts remain 15 g/day =105 g/week.

**Batch prep — first launch should budget real time, not optimistic old estimates**
- Sunday target first-run block: **06:00–07:30**. After two measured runs, shorten only if reality supports it.
  1. Boil all **8 eggs** at once; 2 are Sunday's breakfast, the remaining 6 cover Mon–Sat. Refrigerate in shell.
  2. Cook about **420 g dry pasta** (roughly a 500-g pack minus ~80 g); divide the cooked output into 3 equal Mon/Tue/Wed portions.
  3. Cook roughly **650 g raw chicken** in small uniform pieces using moist covered cooking; aim for about 440 g cooked total. Divide into two larger ~170-g day portions for Mon/Tue and one smaller ~100-g Wednesday portion.
  4. Cook enough red lentils for Mon–Wed dinners; on the very first launch also make Sunday's portion because no prior Wednesday frozen portion exists.
  5. Wash/dry the near-term produce and parsley; do not pre-cut a full week of tomato.
  6. Portion nuts for the week; portion/freeze bread bundles; grind only a small flax batch and keep airtight/cool.
  7. Cool cooked foods promptly in shallow containers, label Mon/Tue/Wed, refrigerate.
- Wednesday target: **19:35–20:50 first runs**, then dinner; if measured real prep becomes reliably faster it can contract.
  1. Cook another ~420 g dry pasta -> Thu/Fri/Sat.
  2. Cook roughly **750 g raw chicken** -> about 510 g cooked, divide into three equal ~170-g daily portions.
  3. Cook four 100-g cooked lentil portions for Thu/Fri/Sat/Sun. Freeze Sunday's portion after cooling; move it to refrigerator Saturday evening.
  4. Refill chopped/washed produce/parsley as needed, clean down, return cold packs to freezer.

**Cold pasta dish**
- Do not overcook to mush, but avoid very firm al dente that becomes unpleasantly hard when cold.
- At morning assembly, mix yogurt + measured olive oil + a few drops lemon + paprika + a little water into a thin dressing; coat pasta/chicken/pepper. Parsley goes in fresh. This prevents dry cold pasta and oil pooling.
- Eat cold with a fork directly from the work refrigerator. No microwave is required.

**Chicken sandwich / 16:45**
- Carry bread separately dry.
- Carry chicken mixed with yogurt/paprika in the chilled container; tomato separately or on top, not against bread all morning.
- Assemble immediately before eating. On training days 80 g white bread is the tactical low-fiber carb portion; on Saturday use Nimet instead.
- Banana is eaten separately. Do not build one oversized soggy sandwich hours in advance.

**Lentil dinner**
- Mash cooked lentils with yogurt, cumin/paprika and a little water. Add olive oil only on days where oil is allocated. This should be a moist spread/bowl, not a dry pile.
- Grate or finely cut carrot if chewing/late-meal volume is annoying. Use Nimet as the dip/scoop where allocated.
- Post-training priority is easy digestion and sleep: do not force a missed earlier meal into this dinner.

**Work transport**
- Morning: take food already refrigerator-cold. Put the pasta container + afternoon chicken/yogurt container adjacent to the **two fully frozen cold packs** inside the backpack; a compact insulated insert is preferred if already available, but do not block launch solely on buying a dedicated bag.
- Dry bread/Etimek/nuts stay separate from wet food; banana/apple/pear stay whole; cut tomato only shortly before eating when practical.
- At work, move **all perishable items immediately to the refrigerator**. Cold packs can remain with the bag/freezer if available.
- 10:00 dry snack does not need the food container; 12:00 lunch and 16:45 filling stay refrigerated until immediately before eating.
- If commute is materially delayed/hot or the work fridge fails, do not 'save' a warmed chicken/yogurt meal for later. Use a fresh-bought/shelf-stable fallback and log what was actually eaten.

**Containers**
- Best practical layout with two work containers:
  - large ~1.0–1.2 L leakproof container = pasta lunch;
  - second smaller leakproof container = chicken+yogurt filling + tomato divider/mini bag.
  - bread/Etimek, nuts and whole fruit ride dry outside the wet containers.
- At night wash/dry both containers; cold packs return to freezer. Next morning fill only with already-cooled food.

**No-scale implementation**
- Pasta: for 140 g/day target, cook ~420 g per 3-day batch. With a 500-g pack, leaving roughly one-sixth (~80 g) is adequate; exact equality is not required.
- Yogurt: 50 g is roughly a repeatable small serving; use the same spoon/portion each time rather than chasing grams. Three 50-g uses/day =150 g target.
- Oat flour: keep the same dedicated spoon/cup each day; consistency matters more than exact density while the 5-kg stock bridge remains.
- Oil: ~10 g ≈ 2 tsp; ~15 g ≈ 1 tbsp; ~20 g ≈ 1 tbsp +1 tsp.
- Bread: use package weight divided by slice count once; then remember slice count per 50/80-g portion.
- Chicken: batch division by relative portions; two large + one small in the first batch, three equal in the second. Bodyweight trend ultimately calibrates the energy error.

**Important packaging correction**
- At only **150 g yogurt/day**, a 3-kg tub would last ~20 days after opening and is no longer the preferred operational pack. Do not carry forward the older 3-kg optimization from the 300-g/day plan.
- Prefer a smaller plain full-fat tub whose label/open-life comfortably covers consumption; **~750 g = ~5 days** is operationally clean, while 1.5 kg = ~10 days should be used only if the physical label and hygiene make that reasonable. Exact brand/price can be optimized at purchase time.
- 1-L UHT milk now lasts ~2.5 days at 400 ml/day; after opening, refrigeration and the package's stated consumption window control.

**Eating ergonomics / first-week validation**
- 12:00 pasta portion is now larger than the old Sep-08 version because pasta increased to 140 g dry/day. The first week must explicitly check: container fit, 30-min eating comfort, post-lunch heaviness and whether 140 g dry is too bulky cold.
- If 140 g dry pasta is repeatedly too bulky, do not force-feed it; the next redesign should redistribute energy to another low-Mn/low-refined source rather than automatically returning to 970 g white bread.
- Taste test rule: if one dish is unpleasant twice, change seasoning/texture/temperature/placement before changing the nutrient architecture.

**Practical packs / reorder rules**
- milk: keep ~3 x1-L available for each coming week; unopened UHT stock can roll forward, so buy to inventory rather than rigidly 3 L every Saturday;
- yogurt: prefer **750-g plain full-fat tubs** operationally; start with 2 tubs (1.5 kg), finish one before opening the next, then rebuy when unopened reserve falls below one tub. A 3-kg tub is obsolete for this intake;
- eggs: 30-pack remains efficient if date/condition comfortably covers ~3.75 weeks;
- chicken: weekly consumption is ~1.4 kg raw; larger freezer stock is acceptable only if unit price and freezer space justify it. Batch-cooking uses ~650 g raw Sunday + ~750 g raw Wednesday;
- pasta: consumption 840 g/week; 2 x500-g packs cover a week with carryover, or bulk dry stock can be used when unit economics are better;
- lentils: 1-kg bag lasts roughly 3+ weeks at ~300 g dry/week;
- Nimet 330-g: need ~2.12 packs/week; buy 3 initially and freeze carryover, then replenish by inventory;
- UNO/base-case white loaf 550-g: need ~1.13 loaves/week; buy 2 initially, freeze excess, then stock-aware reorder;
- Etimek 148-g: 1.5 packs/week; 3 packs cover two weeks;
- sardines: 2 cans/week; 4-pack = about 2 weeks;
- olive oil: 1 L lasts about 4–5 weeks;
- flax: a 200–250 g pack lasts ~6–7 weeks; keep whole/ground product airtight/cool;
- nuts: buy by weight or value pack, but consumption controls remain 240 g almonds +105 g walnuts/week;
- fresh produce: buy weekly; do not bulk-buy beyond realistic freshness.

**Saturday procurement window:** current live Calendar has a paused 19:30–20:00 large-shopping placeholder immediately after the 18:00–19:30 commute. This is usable only if the main store is genuinely on/near the route; otherwise a 30-minute forced store tour is not required. Prefer stock-aware consolidated shopping and monthly dry-stock/freezer purchases over multiple weekly detours.

**Stale live TickTick warning:** read-back on 2026-09-21 confirms the active nutrition project still contains an obsolete high-priority first-shopping checklist with **1500 TL**, rice, mackerel, oranges/cabbage, old yogurt/chicken quantities and old pilot purchased-lunch logic. It also contains a readiness checklist that still asks for a thermobag and thermometer as mandatory-looking steps. These are **not current nutrition instructions**. Do not execute them. Updating/deleting them requires separate explicit live TickTick mutation authorization.

### 2026-09-22 weekly-prep timing correction — remove Sunday-morning deadline
Status: **ASSISTANT-SELECTED WORKING DESIGN / EXECUTION NOT STARTED**.

The earlier heavy Sunday 06:00–07:45 batch is superseded because Sunday library begins at 09:00 while exact home->library travel time is not owned by Calendar. The robust design removes that dependency instead of asking Ron to race an unknown commute.

**Saturday evening after work/shopping**
- Boil all **8 eggs** while dinner/cleanup is happening; refrigerate in shell.
- Prepare the single Sunday breakfast oat portion and refrigerate it.
- Portion dry nuts/bread/Etimek for Sunday/Monday where useful; freeze cold packs.
- This adds only a small mostly-passive task to Saturday and makes Sunday morning low-friction.

**Sunday morning**
- Wake 06:00 as usual; no heavy weekly cooking.
- Reheat/eat the pre-made breakfast with 2 eggs.
- Hygiene/dress/pack the shelf-stable/low-prep library foods.
- Leave according to the actual library travel time; the nutrition system no longer needs to know that travel time in advance.

**Sunday after library — main weekly batch**
- On arriving home, start the main batch; budget **up to ~2 hours on the first run**, not an optimistic fixed 90 minutes.
- Cook all weekly chicken (~1.4 kg raw planning amount), all weekly cooked-lentil target (~700 g), Mon–Wed pasta (~420 g dry), and Mon–Wed oat-breakfast portions.
- Use parallel cooking where actual stove/pan capacity permits; the 2-hour first-run budget includes portioning and initial cleanup.
- Refrigerate Mon–Wed portions; after prompt cooling freeze later-week chicken/lentils. Assemble Monday lunch and Monday dry foods before the 22:00 sleep routine.
- Sunday dinner can use the freshly cooked first lentil portion, so the cooking block and dinner are integrated rather than sequential full tasks.

**Wednesday**
- Short top-up only: Thu–Sat pasta (~420 g dry), Thu–Sat oat breakfasts, produce refresh, and refrigerator transfer of frozen cooked portions. Target ~30–45 min first-run budget; measure reality before shortening.

This design is deliberately more schedule-robust than pre-library heavy prep and does not require knowing the library commute or exact kitchen parallelism before launch.

### 2026-09-22 one-container execution simplification
Status: **ASSISTANT-SELECTED WORKING DESIGN / DAILY TOTALS PRESERVED / NOT YET EXECUTED**.

The earlier work-breakfast workaround is superseded because it silently required another wet-food vessel. Ron has confirmed only the ~1.20-L main container.

**Mon–Sat morning**
- Breakfast stays **at home**, but no cooking from scratch occurs in the 06:00–06:30 window.
- Oat breakfast is batch-cooked and refrigerated. Morning action is only reheat ~2–3 min and eat ~8–10 min with the already-boiled egg.
- Robust minute plan: 06:00 wake/light + toilet/brush; ~06:03 start microwave while dressing; ~06:06–06:16 eat; water rinse; ~06:16–06:25 finish dressing/essentials; ~06:25–06:28 load the already-packed chilled lunch + frozen cold packs + dry foods; ~06:28–06:30 shoes/exit.
- If any morning delay pushes breakfast start beyond ~06:08–06:10, take the boiled egg for later and eat the pre-cooked oat portion quickly rather than missing the 06:30 departure. The system never trades punctuality for finishing every gram at home.

**Single-container work architecture**
- Existing ~1.20-L leakproof container is dedicated to the pasta lunch.
- The 16:45 chicken portion travels chilled in a small food-safe resealable bag/wrap; bread/Nimet, Etimek, nuts and fruit remain dry/outside the main container.
- To remove the need for a second wet container, the **50 g yogurt previously assigned to the 16:45 meal moves to the home dinner on Mon–Sat**. Daily/weekly yogurt totals stay unchanged. Training-day dinner therefore uses ~100 g yogurt instead of 50 g; Wednesday and Saturday dinners likewise absorb the moved 50 g.
- Wednesday 16:45 becomes Etimek + whole/firm tomato + banana, with no yogurt dip. Saturday 16:45 becomes Nimet + chilled chicken + tomato + banana, no yogurt.
- This is a timing/packaging redistribution only; it does not intentionally change daily energy or weekly food totals.

**Training-night clock**
- Ron reports gym -> home <=5 min. Use **20:55 exercise hard-stop**, ~5 min to change/exit, ~21:05 home.
- Pre-made dinner: ~21:07–21:20; shower ~21:20–21:30; next-day load/containers ~21:30–21:40; hygiene/wind-down ~21:40–22:00.
- Ron directly confirms the current program was already optimized for practical execution, including supersets/circuits. The old 145-min active-program session predates later planner/source changes, so it is not evidence that the current optimized version fails the evening window. Nutrition must not impose a new short-session fallback by default; preserve the existing training design and reassess only from new post-optimization execution evidence.

### 2026-09-22 direct execution constraints — container and gym-home transit
Status: **DIRECT RON FACTS / CURRENT FOR NUTRITION EXECUTION**.

- Ron reports his main food container is approximately **1.20 L**.
- Ron reports **gym -> home travel is no more than ~5 minutes**.
- Consequence: the ~600–650 g planned pasta lunch is physically plausible in the existing ~1.20-L container with useful headspace; no larger lunch container is currently required.
- Consequence: on training nights, a **21:00 gym finish -> ~21:05 home arrival** is compatible with a 22:00 sleep target if dinner is already prepared and next-day packing remains minimal. The schedule no longer needs an automatic ~20:50 training hard-stop purely for travel time.

### 2026-09-22 schedule-feasibility correction — assistant-selected execution redesign
Status: **ASSISTANT-SELECTED WORKING DESIGN / NOT YET RON-EXECUTED / DAILY NUTRIENT TOTALS UNCHANGED**.

Trigger: Ron directly pointed out that a nominal 06:00 breakfast conflicts with the real 06:30 departure. Live Calendar independently confirms 06:00 wake, 06:30 departure, 07:30 work start, 18:00 work end, ~18:00–19:30 commute, Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri training slot 19:30–21:00, 22:00 sleep target, and Sunday library 09:00–18:00.

**Morning correction, Mon–Sat**
- Do not schedule cooking, wet-food assembly or a sit-down full breakfast inside the 30-minute wake-to-departure window.
- Assistant-selected robust default: batch-cook the oat-flour breakfast, chill it, carry it cold with the boiled egg, and eat it at work around **07:35–07:50**. Ron has previously reported that he can eat at work and the work refrigerator is the food-holding path.
- 06:00–06:30 is reserved for waking, toilet/hygiene, dressing, loading already-packed chilled containers + two frozen cold packs, and leaving at 06:30.
- Work meal clocks become approximately **07:35 breakfast / 10:30 snack / 13:00 lunch / 16:45 pre-training or afternoon meal**. This improves spacing and removes the brittle 06:00 meal assumption.
- If cold oat pudding is unacceptable, fallback is a fully pre-made home breakfast with a **06:10 start cutoff**; if not eating by 06:10, take it to work rather than risk the 06:30 departure.

**Training-day feasibility**
- 18:00 departure from work must route directly toward the gym on Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri; going home first and then starting at 19:30 is not a robust default.
- The current Calendar 19:30–21:00 gym block leaves almost no slack before 22:00 sleep. Operational rule: choose a hard stop that still permits gym->home transit, a pre-made dinner, hygiene, next-day packing and lights-out. If gym->home is ~10 min, **~20:50** is the practical target hard-stop; longer transit moves the stop earlier.
- This does not silently redesign the training program. The training owner already records that the current program can exceed the calendar slot and has a short-session minimum. If repeated full-program duration does not fit the hard stop, that is a separate training/schedule redesign problem rather than a reason to sacrifice sleep or nutrition execution by default.

**Batch architecture correction**
- Sunday becomes the main production block. To reduce Wednesday fatigue/cognitive load, cook **all weekly chicken (~1.4 kg raw planning amount)** and **all weekly lentils (~700 g cooked target)** on Sunday; refrigerate early-week portions and freeze Thu–Sun portions after prompt cooling.
- Sunday also cooks the Mon–Wed pasta batch (~420 g dry), all 8 boiled eggs, and the Sun–Wed breakfast oat batch. Budget roughly **06:05–07:45** for the first real run, using parallel cooking where equipment allows.
- Wednesday top-up becomes materially smaller: cook only Thu–Sat pasta (~420 g dry), Thu–Sat breakfast oat portions, refresh produce as needed, and move the next frozen cooked portions to the refrigerator. Target design block **~19:35–20:15**, then dinner. If real equipment makes this slower, the first measured run owns the next revision.
- Freezing later cooked chicken/lentils is deliberately preferred over repeating a full Wednesday chicken/lentil cook because the reduction in worknight burden dominates the modest texture cost; yogurt/sauce is already part of the serving design.

**Salt rule**
- Exact historical discretionary-salt behavior is UNKNOWN; do not pretend it was known.
- Assistant-selected launch control is a simple **weekly iodized-table-salt budget of about 2 level teaspoons/week** (roughly ~10–12 g/week depending crystal density, ~1.4–1.7 g/day average), used mainly for pasta/chicken/lentils and preferably added late/after cooking where practical.
- At the current donor baseline ~1,218 mg sodium/day, this adds roughly ~560–680 mg sodium/day, giving a modeled total around **~1,780–1,900 mg/day**, still near/below the WHO <2,000 mg/day population target before donor/product error.
- This salt budget helps taste and contributes iodine but is **not** treated as proof of iodine adequacy; Turkish table salt is iodized by regulation, but exact retained iodine depends on product and handling.

**Fiber prior rather than artificial uncertainty**
- ~44.2 g/day at ~3131 kcal is ~14.1 g/1000 kcal.
- Pre-launch assistant judgment: in an otherwise healthy young adult with no reported GI restriction, mixed food-source fiber at this density is more likely than not to be tolerated without a material problem. A rough decision-model prior is **~75–85% chance of no material GI problem**, **~15–25% chance of noticeable but usually manageable transient gas/fullness/stool change**, and **low single-digit probability of a problem severe enough to require abandoning the design**, conditional on no undisclosed GI disease and acknowledging that the exact current baseline fiber intake is unknown.
- These are decision-model probabilities, not frequencies measured in Ron-specific trials. Real week-1 observations update the prior but do not keep the launch design open.

### 2026-09-21 vitamin-D closure after current evidence/product check
Status: **ASSISTANT-SELECTED DESIGN BASELINE / NOT PURCHASED / NOT STARTED**.

- Current evidence check confirms that generally healthy adults under 50 should meet the ~600 IU/day (15 µg/day) DRI and should not routinely escalate above the DRI without a separate indication; routine 25(OH)D screening is not required solely to set a preventive dose in this population.
- Read-back of the exact clean Cronometer planned week (2026-10-05..11) gives **~169 IU/day food-only vitamin D on average**, superseding the earlier ~282-IU estimate. A **400 IU/day D3** top-up would give a donor-model total of ~**569 IU/day**. The remaining ~31 IU/day difference from the 600-IU RDA is small relative to product/database uncertainty and is not worth extra dose complexity; the adult UL remains 4,000 IU/day.
- **K2 is not automatically required with this low-dose D3 plan.** The current food model already passed broad micronutrient adequacy screening, and current authoritative evidence does not establish a routine K2-supplement requirement for healthy adults merely because vitamin D is used.
- Practical product spec: simple **D3 (cholecalciferol), 400 IU per repeatable adult-labeled serving**, preferably oil-based, without unnecessary extra vitamins/minerals. Natuwell D3 400 IU is one currently verified example whose official label allows adults 11+ to use 1–2 puffs; **1 puff = 400 IU**. Exact product remains replaceable at purchase time if another product matches the same spec and has better current total-system ROI.
- This does **not** authorize or imply purchase/intake. Food-plan launch does not depend on the supplement being in hand.

Evidence checked 2026-09-21: Endocrine Society 2024 vitamin D guideline; NIH Office of Dietary Supplements vitamin D and vitamin K fact sheets; current Natuwell official D3 400 IU product page.

### 2026-09-21 vitamin-D consistency correction
Status: **FOOD PLAN DOES NOT CLAIM D3 HAS STARTED; TARGET IS TOTAL INTAKE, NOT A MAGIC SUPPLEMENT DOSE**.

- Current Turkish-donor food model is ~**282 IU/day vitamin D**. Adult RDA for generally healthy adults <50 remains **600 IU/day**; the 2024 Endocrine Society guideline advises against empiric supplementation *above* the DRI in this population and against routine 25(OH)D screening in otherwise healthy adults without another indication.
- Therefore the useful target is to cover the approximate **~318 IU/day food gap**, not to insist on a particular branded supplement.
- The modeled **400 IU/day D3** is a reasonable planning amount to bring total intake to ~682 IU/day, but it is **not yet actual intake and not a purchase/execution fact**.
- The older 600-IU spray candidate should not silently replace the 400-IU model. If a 400-IU formulation is convenient, it is the cleaner match. If only a 600-IU unit is available, re-evaluate the schedule/product rather than automatically taking 600 IU daily just because it is sold that way.
- Vitamin D is not a blocker to starting the food-prep system; food execution can start while the exact low-dose formulation remains pending.

### 2026-09-23 low-friction functional-adjunct root added
Status: **RON-REQUESTED CLASS / ASSISTANT-SELECTED INITIAL GREEN-TEA RULE**.

- Ron explicitly requested adding green tea and, more importantly, the reusable root for other similarly cheap/easy potentially useful food-level bioactives.
- The durable factor map now contains a dedicated **Low-friction functional adjuncts / beverages / culinary bioactives** screen instead of relying on the broader bioactive paragraph to catch these incidentally.
- Initial green-tea execution rule: use unsweetened green tea as optional workday support, primarily in the first half of the day; an optional second cup must stay early enough not to compromise the ~22:00 sleep target. Do not treat evening caffeinated green tea as a sleep intervention.
- Tea is kept roughly >=1 h away from iron-relevant meals when practical because controlled human evidence shows simultaneous tea reduces non-heme iron absorption and a 1-h interval attenuates that inhibition.
- Evidence interpretation stays bounded: current randomized-trial syntheses support small, uncertain cognition/attention/mood benefits from tea/L-theanine/caffeine combinations; they do not justify a blanket "memory enhancer" claim or an L-theanine accumulation mechanism.
- This root is a **screen**, not a mandate to add many ingredients. Future candidates must beat their added cost/friction/interaction risk and deliver a plausible food-level dose before entering the baseline.


### 2026-09-23 first functional-adjunct sweep completed
Status: **SCREEN COMPLETE / ONE NEW RECURRING ITEM / NO STACK**.

- The new low-friction root was run across green/black tea and coffee, cocoa, culinary herbs/spices, garlic/ginger/turmeric/cinnamon-type additions, lemon/citrus, vinegar, fermented drinks/foods and mineral-water variants.
- **Green tea won the new-recurring-item screen.** Assistant-selected working rule: unsweetened, 1 cup around 08:30–09:00 on workdays; optional second around ~13:00 only when useful. This aligns with the current ~10:00 and ~12:00 meal pattern so tea can remain roughly >=1 h away from iron-relevant meals, and keeps caffeine far from the ~22:00 sleep target. If sleep latency/quality worsens, remove the second cup first.
- Current evidence supports small/uncertain cognition/attention/mood benefits from tea/L-theanine/caffeine combinations, while caffeine can impair subsequent sleep depending on dose/timing. This is a modest performance/health lever, not a memory drug or sleep treatment.
- **Lemon and spices were already operationally present**, so no new food mass is needed: current pasta dressing already uses lemon + paprika, with curry-yogurt as fallback. Use culinary herbs/spices primarily for taste, variety and lower reliance on salt; do not impose supplement-like doses.
- Vitamin-C-rich food with plant-iron meals can improve meal-level non-heme iron absorption, but whole-diet impact is smaller than single-meal studies suggest. Therefore lemon/citrus is a low-friction helper, not a required therapeutic intervention.
- **Not promoted to baseline after the first sweep:** coffee alongside tea; cocoa as a daily intervention; medicinal-dose garlic/ginger/turmeric/cinnamon; vinegar dosing; extra kefir/kombucha; special mineral water. Main reasons are redundancy, uncertain actual food-level dose, evidence concentrated in supplements/high-risk populations, added calories/cost/friction, or marginal incremental value over the existing diet.
- This closes the first pass. Future candidates are screened through the durable root rather than reopening an unbounded search for 'healthy substances'.

### 2026-09-23 TickTick minimal-cognition execution set — exact proposed live delta
Status: **LIVE WRITE COMPLETED / VERIFIED READ-BACK**.

This is the assistant-selected minimum useful automation set after Calendar + current runbook reconciliation. It is deliberately smaller than "remind every meal" to reduce notification competition while still externalizing the steps most likely to require memory.

1. **Sat 2026-09-26 20:00 — Day 0: eggs + launch staging**
   - Boil 8 eggs; cool and refrigerate in shell.
   - Freeze both cold packs.
   - Stage the next breakfast dry oat portion in the microwave-safe bowl/container.
   - Ensure Monday dry foods / food-safe wrap or bag / clean ~1.20-L container are ready.
   - Reminder at task time; Europe/Istanbul.

2. **Sun 2026-09-27 18:00 — After library: first Mon–Wed batch**
   - Trigger is intentionally "after returning home"; 18:00 is the earliest cue, not an assumption that Ron is already home.
   - Cook ~650 g raw chicken for Mon–Wed.
   - Cook full ~700 g cooked-lentil weekly batch; keep ~400 g Sun–Wed refrigerated, freeze ~300 g Thu–Sat after cooling.
   - Cook ~420 g dry pasta for Mon–Wed.
   - Cool promptly in shallow/smaller portions; refrigerate Mon–Wed food.
   - Build Monday lunch + dry-food pack before 21:20 wind-down.
   - Record prep start/finish and whether cleanup/storage fit caused trouble.
   - Do not sacrifice 22:00 sleep for paper-perfect prep.

3. **Mon–Sat 06:00 during launch week — breakfast + load + leave 06:30**
   - Add ~250 ml milk to staged ~80 g oat portion; microwave ~2–3 min.
   - Add allocated flax/pekmez; eat with boiled egg.
   - Load chilled lunch + 16:45 chicken portion + dry foods + both frozen cold packs.
   - Leave 06:30; do not measure/search from scratch in the morning.
   - First-week recurring task only; reassess after execution evidence.

4. **Mon–Sat 07:30 during launch week — food -> work refrigerator**
   - Put all chicken/dairy/pasta-containing food directly into the work refrigerator on arrival.
   - If fridge is unavailable/warm or cold-chain rule is breached, use safe fallback rather than eating the planned perishable meal.
   - This is kept separate because it is a safety-critical arrival action.

5. **Sun–Fri 21:20 during launch week — next-day staging + EOD reconciliation**
   - Stage next morning's ~80 g oat portion.
   - Freeze both cold packs.
   - Ensure next-day chilled portions/dry snack are ready; do not leave morning measuring/searching.
   - Reconcile the day to reality: if planned food was changed/missed/replaced, preserve the actual fact rather than assuming the prefilled Cronometer plan happened.
   - Note only material week-1 failures: morning delay, prep/cleanup burden, storage fit, lunch >30 min/fullness, 16:45 palatability, GI issue, cold-chain issue, backpack discomfort, late-dinner/sleep issue.

6. **Wed 2026-09-30 19:45 — Thu–Sat top-up**
   - Cook ~750 g raw chicken for Thu/Fri/Sat.
   - Cook ~420 g dry pasta for Thu–Sat.
   - Move frozen ~300 g lentil portion to refrigerator.
   - No second lentil cook and no oat-breakfast batch.
   - Record active/elapsed time; preserve 22:00 sleep target.

**Deliberately not in the minimum set yet:** separate 10:00/12:00/16:45 meal alerts and green-tea alerts. They remain unnecessary unless actual misses show that meal timing itself is a memory problem; this avoids notification spam.

All timed tasks should use explicit `Europe/Istanbul`. The unchangeable TickTick profile `Asia/Ashgabat` metadata is ignored per integration contract; concrete task read-back owns timing correctness.
