### 2026-09-23 taste / real-edibility / cooking audit
Status: **DESIGN RECHECKED / TECHNIQUE CORRECTIONS ADOPTED / REAL TASTE STILL EMPIRICAL**.

The current menu is not a "nutrient spreadsheet that cannot be eaten"; most meals are ordinary workable foods. However, the audit found that **technique**, not ingredients, is the main determinant of whether the plan feels normal or punishing.

**1. Breakfast — oat-flour porridge**
- Main risk: ~80 g fine oat flour + only ~250 ml milk can turn into a dense glue/paste after microwave heating.
- Working method: whisk the dry oat flour with ~250 ml milk **plus ~80–120 ml water to start** before heating; microwave ~60–90 s, stir thoroughly, then another ~60–90 s as needed. Add more water after heating if it thickens while standing.
- Add 5 g flax + 10 g pekmez after cooking. A pinch of iodized salt and/or cinnamon is culinary-only and optional.
- Use a bowl comfortably larger than the wet mixture to reduce boil-over.
- Success criterion: spoonable porridge that can be eaten in ~8–10 min without force-feeding. If still unpleasant twice, coarser rolled oats become the preferred replacement once the owned flour stock is exhausted.

**2. Cold pasta lunch**
- Expected sensory profile is viable: creamy-acidic yogurt/lemon dressing, olive-oil richness, paprika/herb aroma, mild chicken, crisp pepper.
- Main risk is refrigerated pasta/chicken becoming firm and dry.
- Cook pasta fully tender but not mushy; very firm al dente becomes harder when chilled. Drain promptly and spread/portion rather than leaving a hot dense mass.
- Morning dressing for each normal 20-g-oil lunch: existing 50 g yogurt + 20 g olive oil + lemon + paprika + ~1–2 tbsp water, mixed into an emulsion before coating pasta/chicken/pepper. Wednesday uses its existing lower oil allotment, with water adjusted for the same coating texture.
- Chicken should be small-cut/shredded and carry a little retained cooking juice into the portion. Parsley is added fresh.
- Container estimate remains plausible: roughly ~600–650 g finished lunch should fit the known ~1.20-L box with useful headspace, but actual fit/eating comfort is a week-1 observation.

**3. Chicken — critical anti-dryness rule**
- Breast is the biggest taste-risk food in the architecture.
- Prefer uniform 2–3 cm pieces or strips, ideally cut by the seller. Season with curry/paprika/black pepper and normal iodized salt within the weekly ceiling.
- In a nonstick pan/pot, add only a small splash of water, cover, cook at moderate heat, stir/turn enough for even heating, and stop once the **largest/thickest piece reaches 74°C**. Rest covered ~5 min.
- Do not chase browning/crust at the expense of moisture. Retain some clean cooking juice and distribute it with the chicken portions.
- This method intentionally optimizes cold next-day texture rather than restaurant-style searing.

**4. 16:45 work meal**
- This is currently the weakest sensory slot after moving yogurt home.
- Training days: 80 g white bread + 70 g chicken + 50 g tomato + banana. To keep it edible, finely chop/shred the chicken while still moist, store it with a small amount of retained cooking juice, and keep tomato/bread separate until eating. Lemon/paprika/pepper can season the chicken without changing calories.
- Wednesday Etimek and Saturday Nimet variants are inherently drier; fruit/tomato provide moisture, and water should be available. Do not force a dry assembly if it repeatedly fails: two poor exposures trigger a redesign of the moisture carrier/portion placement rather than a willpower solution.
- This slot remains the most likely recipe to require empirical revision.

**5. Lentil-yogurt dinners**
- Best real dish is not a pile of separate ingredients. Reheat lentils until warm, then mix/fold with yogurt after heating; add cumin + dried mint + paprika/black pepper, olive oil at the end. Grated carrot can be mixed in or eaten alongside; tomato/pepper stay fresh for contrast.
- Nimet/bread stays separate for texture.
- This should be moist, spoonable and substantially easier to eat than dry lentils + bread.

**6. Sardine meals**
- Do not mix sardines into the yogurt-lentil bowl.
- Eat the whole Dardanel can as its own component with lemon/tomato and Nimet/Etimek; use the can's oil to moisten bites of bread/rusk. Lentil-yogurt bowl and milk are separate components.
- Sunday ~100 g Etimek is a high-dryness/high-chewing item despite the sardine oil; it is a specific week-1 palatability/eating-time check.

**7. Batch workflow**
- If at least two burners + one pan/pot are available, Sunday can overlap lentils, pasta and chicken; if not, elapsed time may materially exceed the current ~60–90-min estimate.
- Portion hot food into shallow/small containers soon after cooking and refrigerate promptly; safe cooling does not require waiting for everything to become room-temperature in a deep pot.
- Wednesday is simpler because lentils are not recooked.
- Real burner count/pan size/storage capacity remain OPEN-EMPIRICAL rather than guessed.

**8. Cognitive-load equipment**
- A ~1-g digital kitchen scale now has unusually high system value because the menu repeats many small exact quantities and two ~420-g pasta batches. It is not a nutritional or safety prerequisite, but without it the plan relies on package fractions and household-volume approximations, which conflicts with Ron's stated objective of keeping as little as possible in working memory.

**Expected pre-launch verdict:** breakfast **good if thinned correctly**; cold pasta **likely good/normal**; lentil-yogurt dinner **likely good when treated as an actual seasoned bowl**; sardine meals **acceptable but distinctive**; 16:45 dry-carb/chicken/Etimek slots are the main palatability risk. No nutrient-architecture change is justified before tasting the real prepared food, but the cooking/moisture techniques above should be treated as the current recipe baseline.

### 2026-09-23 final food-safety instrument gap
Status: **DESIGN DEFECT FOUND / LIVE SYNC REQUIRES EXPLICIT AUTHORIZATION**.

- Current chicken method correctly targets ~74°C / 165°F internal temperature, but Ron does not own a food thermometer.
- Current USDA/FSIS guidance states that a food thermometer is the only sure way to verify poultry has reached a sufficiently high internal temperature; appearance/texture/juice cues are not reliable safety tests.
- Therefore the existing TickTick phrase "stop at 74°C" is not fully executable as written until an instant-read food thermometer is available.
- High-ROI correction: add an inexpensive instant-read food thermometer to the launch procurement reminder. A basic ~1-g digital kitchen scale should be listed alongside it as a strong cognitive-load/portioning recommendation, but it is not a safety prerequisite.
- Once the thermometer exists, chicken task wording should explicitly say: measure the thickest/largest piece; do not stop before >=74°C; then rest covered for texture.
- Until that live delta is authorized + read back, automation row #28 remains STALE-LIVE rather than falsely PASS-LIVE.

### 2026-09-23 concrete D3 product + K2 decision
Status: **ASSISTANT-SELECTED / LIVE PURCHASE + DAILY REMINDER SYNCHRONIZED**.

- Concrete default chosen: **Youplus Vitamin D3 400 IU 20 ml Oral Sprey (Abdi İbrahim)**; manufacturer lists 400 IU D3 per oral spray. Current online reference found: Nar Ecza ~50 TL, with other online sellers varying materially.
- Execution: once physically purchased, **1 puff daily at 12:00 with/after lunch**, which already contains dietary fat on the current menu. TickTick daily reminder id: `6ab3b5eb8f080aea1acba8ef`.
- K2 correction: K2 is not an absorption cofactor required for vitamin D. NIH ODS states that intestinal vitamin-D absorption is enhanced by concurrent dietary fat; vitamin K instead functions in gamma-carboxylation of vitamin-K-dependent proteins. Evidence for routine K supplementation to improve bone/cardiovascular outcomes is mixed/uncertain, and clinically significant vitamin-K deficiency is rare in healthy adults eating varied diets.
- Therefore the default remains D3-only rather than paying for/forcing a combo. If a future K-specific indication appears, reassess K independently.
- For comparison only, Youplus D3K2 is an official 1000-IU D3 + 20-µg K2 product; choosing it would increase the planned D dose from 400 to 1000 IU and is not adopted merely to obtain K2.


### 2026-09-23 Ron chose D3+K2 combined regimen
Status: **RON DECISION / ASSISTANT PRODUCT SELECTION / LIVE SYNCHRONIZED**.

- Ron explicitly preferred **D3 + K2 together** because dietary K2 coverage is not well established and the K2 amount in combination products is modest.
- This supersedes the prior assistant-selected D3-only plan.

### 2026-09-23 D3K2 bioavailability recheck -> spray chosen
Status: **RON D3+K2 DECISION UNCHANGED / ASSISTANT FORM CHOICE UPDATED / LIVE SYNCHRONIZED**.

- Rechecked absorption/bioavailability rather than assuming liquid forms are universally superior.
- Human evidence is mixed: standard oral D3 spray and capsules have shown equivalent 25(OH)D responses in randomized trials; oil drops and tablets have also been bioequivalent. A small crossover trial reported greater response from buccal spray, including in malabsorption, but this does not establish a universal class effect. Special nanoemulsion D3 can have higher AUC/Cmax than conventional softgels, but Nutraxin D3K2 Spray is an olive-oil solution, not a demonstrated nanoemulsion.
- For K2, MK-7 itself is well absorbed and long-lived; formulation/meal context can alter bioavailability, but no direct Nutraxin spray-vs-tablet MK-7 trial was found.
- Nutraxin official data confirm that both spray and tablet provide **D3 1000 IU + K2 MK-7 25 µg**. The spray carrier is **extra virgin olive oil**.
- Current Nar Ecza listing: ~207 puffs at ~233.36 TL; current tablet price anchors are roughly ~204–244 TL for 120 tablets. Thus spray currently offers materially more daily doses for a similar total price.
- **Assistant product/form decision updated:** choose **Nutraxin Vitamin D3K2 Sprey 30 ml**, 1 puff/day, instead of the 120-tablet version. This is a total-ROI choice, not a claim that spray intrinsically absorbs better in every person.
- TickTick Friday procurement and daily 12:00 reminder were updated/read back.

### 2026-09-25 execution-format rule — grams + practical equivalents
- **Direct Ron request:** all nutrition instructions should show the exact mass/volume **and** a practical human-readable equivalent whenever feasible, so execution does not require mentally converting grams.
- Format rule: `exact grams/ml first -> practical equivalent second`.
- Examples of preferred equivalents by food type:
  - bread: grams + approximate slice count, with slice thickness/size once calibrated to the actual loaf;
  - milk/water: ml + glass/cup fraction;
  - yogurt: grams + tablespoons or a fraction of the actual tub;
  - olive oil: grams + tsp/tbsp;
  - nuts: grams + approximate nut count/handful only after actual-size calibration where useful;
  - fruit/vegetables: grams + number of small/medium/large pieces or fractions;
  - eggs/cans/packaged units: grams where relevant + whole units;
  - pasta/lentils/oats: dry grams + pack fraction / repeatable household scoop after calibration;
  - chicken/fish: grams + whole-piece/palm/portion equivalent only when the physical portion is sufficiently repeatable.
- **Precision rule:** grams/ml remain the canonical nutrition quantity. Practical equivalents are execution aids and must not be presented as exact when product size/density varies.
- **Calibration rule:** prefer one cheap calibration of Ron's real products (e.g. weigh one bread slice, one tablespoon of yogurt/oil, one typical fruit) over generic internet averages. Once calibrated, reuse the same practical equivalent consistently in meal instructions and reminders until the product/utensil changes.
- For the 2026-09-24 bakery sourdough loaf, exact weight is still unverified, so do not invent an exact slice-count equivalent yet; use loaf fractions only as a clearly approximate temporary aid.

## 2026-11-03 — BUY-02 Pro+ retail label contradiction: reject 26g masquerading as 52g

**Material purchasing/meal-identity hazard (new evidence, not mere theoretical branch):** Pınar's [official Pro+ formulation](https://www.pinarprotein.com/protein-plus) specifies black-package **500ml Pro+ = 52g protein**, **10.4g/100ml**, **66 kcal/100ml = 330 kcal/carton**, calcium **180mg/100ml = 900mg/carton**; the separate [official vanilla ordinary Protein Milk](https://www.pinarprotein.com/vanilya-aromali-sut) is **500ml = 26g protein**, **5.2g/100ml**, **47 kcal/100ml = 235 kcal/carton**, also calcium 900mg/carton. Both are distinct from 1L 52g variants. Exact full-carton difference 26g protein and 95 kcal. This difference can invalidate the B2 dinner/full-day protein target if retailer product title alone is believed.

[Özdilekteyim](https://www.ozdilekteyim.com/market/pinar-sut-protein-pro-500-ml) titles product 'Pınar Süt Protein Pro+ 500 Ml' but its public nutrition panel directly below states **5.2g protein and 47 kcal per100ml** — the **26g ordinary** profile, NOT the 52g manufacturer Pro+ formula. This is a demonstrable *retailer listing/title vs label inconsistency*, not proof the shelf unit is 26g. A [Trendyol 24-pack seller Q&A](https://www.trendyol.com/pinar/yuksek-proteinli-laktozsuz-sut-500-ml-x-24-adet-p-1142725991) contains contradictory seller answers (26g and 52g for nominally same listing), so marketplace metadata/answers cannot decide package identity.

## 2026-11-03 — BUY-02 exact Yayla150 kcal/kJ display transposition

**New concrete upstream label fault, high-impact if ingested literally:** The exact [Migros supplier-facing Yayla Suyu Süzülmüş Nohut 150g page](https://www.migros.com.tr/hemen/yayla-haslanmis-suyu-suzulmus-nohut-150-g-p-89e07d) publicly prints **Enerji (kcal) = 582.0** and **Enerji (kJ) = 139.0** in its per-100-g nutrition table. These **units are reversed**: 582 kJ ÷ 4.184 = **139.10 kcal**; 139 kcal × 4.184 = **581.6 kJ**. Treat `582 kcal/100g` as a **demonstrably impossible unit interpretation** of this internally consistent pair, not as a 4.2× energy increase. Correct interpretation **~139 kcal/100g**, matching the pre-existing B2 ingredient ledger, gives **~208.5 kcal per 150g pouch** rather than erroneous **873 kcal per pouch**. Other exact Migros-listed per-100-g macros are 7.8g protein, 16.8g carbohydrate, 2.0g fat, 0.3g SFA and 0.1g salt, corroborating that 582 kcal is implausible for that declared macronutrient quantity.

**Impact prevented and regression:** Blindly parsing the listing as 582 kcal/100g would overcount one B2 lunchtime pouch by **664.5 kcal**, falsely inflating the already-modelled ~2863 kcal Pro52 B2 day to ~3527.5 kcal/day even with all quantities unchanged, and three identical B2 days by **1993.5 kcal**. Corrected kJ↔kcal interpretation preserves previously recorded **208.5 kcal per pouch / ~2863 kcal conditional whole B2 day** and leaves protein, saturated fat, sodium, 3-day fiber estimates and all recipes unchanged. This is a **source-data correction**, NOT a nutritional plan modification or assertion of current purchase. If the actual future physical packet shows a different nutrient panel, the physical label supersedes the retailer's erroneous units; current store stock and checkout remain UNKNOWN. Existing 32.97 TRY Migros Money promotion is a separate dated offer, not affected by unit correction. BUY-02 partial source integrity check complete; full task remains OPEN.

## NUT-02 / 2026-11-03 — conditional 3-day B2 choline and egg-rescue model

**New actionable micro-to-meal screen; NOT an adopted B diet or actual intake.** For the existing nonfish 5-slot B2 recipe (06 UHT milk200ml/bread100/banana120/almond15/flax5;10 almond15/walnut15/apple180/bread40;12 chickpeas150/bread100/tomato150/pepper100/parsley15/EVOO15;16:45 UHTmilk200/bread80/banana120;home dinner exact Pro+52g500ml/bread80/carrot100/tomato150/EVOO20), read-only connected Cronometer food-details-batch resolved NCCDB/USDA donor choline for all edible foods at these grams. **Without Pro+ (no exact Pınar choline label): ~285.31mg choline/day**, including whole-milk 400g donor for the two200ml UHT cartons (not an exact density/brand conversion). For the **unreported** choline of Pro+ only, a clearly hypothetical 500g generic whole-milk proxy supplies +89mg (17.8mg/100g), making **~374.31mg/day**. This proxy is NOT a measured Pro+ assay: the true whole-day B2 choline remains UNKNOWN; product/label/donor variation could change it. [NIH ODS official reference](https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/Choline-HealthProfessional/) sets adult male AI=550mg/day; AI is NOT an RDA, and falling below it in a short model does not diagnose deficiency. At that 374.31mg proxy the model sits ~175.69mg below AI, a credible recurring B2 risk worth handling before 2–3 consecutive B days.

**Concrete conditional, no-new-special-product option:** ONLY if existing A-stock **two already hard-boiled eggs, 100g edible** are genuinely surplus (not stealing tomorrow's A servings), can be kept safely refrigerated at home and eaten at the late home dinner, add those 100g to the B2 Pro+/bread/vegetables meal. Independent Cronometer NCCDB464674 and USDA1239 agreed per100g: **155kcal,12.58g protein,10.61g fat,3.267g SFA,293.8mg choline,1.11µg B12,87 IU D**. With the explicit Pro+ choline proxy only, B2 choline would become **~668.11mg**; this demonstrates the order of magnitude, NOT a product-exact adequacy certification. The previously preserved two-nut label-calibrated nonfish B2 Pro52 layer **2859.10kcal/120.3365g protein/87.5745g fat** would become **3014.10kcal/132.9165g protein/98.1845g fat**, SFA existing 22.25485–22.29985 becomes25.52185–25.56685g. Compared with adopted A label-calibrated ~3060–3080kcal/~145g protein, this conditional nonfish dinner gets closer but does NOT restore protein or all micros, and product packaging still gates Pro52. If the *same egg addition* were available on each day of the existing one-fish/two-nonfish **three-day B2 model**, daily-average **2913.2167kcal/126.3992g protein/92.4905g fat** changes to **3068.2167kcal/138.9792g protein/103.1005g fat**, conditional on 300g edible boiled egg supply (~6 large eggs) and no A inventory displacement. **No recommendation to add six eggs by default**: purchase cash, fat/cholesterol context, late dinner fullness/GI/sleep, preparation/storage time and 3-day surplus are unknown. Fish-day full micros and B2 iodine, D, EPA+DHA, real product calcium still need independent validation; extra eggs do not erase that requirement.

**Execution gate:** This is a *candidate rescue for repeated B2* if and only if safe surplus home eggs exist; not a requirement for a one-off missed meal. If eggs absent, leave choline UNKNOWN/risk flagged instead of prescribing unsafe unrefrigerated eggs or another compulsory shopping trip. The no-microwave work lunch remains unchanged; never take chilled eggs to campus without cold chain. B1 already contains eggs at breakfast and must not inherit this B2 addition. No physical purchase, diet A/Cronometer entry, Calendar, reminders or TEST-01 execution claim. NUT-02 and NUT-01 remain OPEN pending comprehensive multi-day micros, actual source labels and empirical consumption.

**04Nov2026 NUT03 — B1 packed lunch specific cold-chain acceptance gate (new official USDA FSIS detail, no new equipment purchase):** USDA's official [How do you keep a bag lunch safe?](https://ask.fsis.usda.gov/article/How-do-you-keep-a-bag-lunch-safe) specifies **at least TWO frozen cold sources**, e.g. **two gel packs each at least 5×3 inches (~12.7×7.62cm)**, overnight-frozen and positioned **one above and one below the perishable food** in an **insulated bag**. Ron already owns two cold packs, but their size and actual placement and temperature endurance are **UNKNOWN**: 'two packs' alone is NOT a verified B1 chilled meal solution, especially morning→12:00 work transport and a hot commute. Independently [FDA](https://www.fda.gov/consumers/consumer-updates/are-you-storing-food-safely) and [CDC](https://www.cdc.gov/food-safety/prevention/) set refrigerated food **≤4°C (40°F)** and a maximum **2h cumulatively out of temperature control** (**1h** in ambient >32°C/90°F). For a B1 meal assembled around06:00 and eaten12:00, **six hours unverified ambient transport would exceed 2h by four hours**; ice packs can preserve cold only when actual food temperature is kept ≤4°C, not just because the packs remain present/cool to the touch. Operational conditional gate: pre-chill sealed perishable components, freeze both gel packs fully, bag insulated top+bottom, use actual fridge at work immediately if confirmed ≤4°C; otherwise validate **the FOOD itself ≤4°C at eating time** with a food thermometer or reject the perishable B1 and use a fully unopened shelf-stable B2, with produce/components prepared only at consumption. If time above4°C cannot be bounded (or exceeds2h, 1h hot ambient), do not eat that unrefrigerated chilled dairy/chicken/egg-based B1. A single spot temperature measurement cannot reconstruct an unknown earlier warm excursion; empirical time-temperature check is still OPEN. This is a **specific safety/eligibility gate**, not evidence Ron's actual lunch is unsafe or that the present packs fail. Work-fridge actual availability/temperature, gel pack size, bag insulation, transit and test are UNKNOWN. Specific producer information for [Legurme/Mediterranean 400g identical 55% formula](https://yaylastore.com/legurme-mediterranean-haslanmis-meksika-fasulyesi-400-gr) confirms 220g drained, ambient sealed, opened +4°C ≤2d; exact Yayla retailer [Migros Toptan09035878](https://toptan.migros.com.tr/yayla-haslanmis-meksika-fasulyesi-400-g-p-09035878) confirms 55% beans and nutrition (per100g **117kcal/9gP/13gC/1.4gF/8.3g fiber/0.5g salt**), but **does not state whether values are for wet400g as-packed or drained220g**; do NOT multiply it by 4 or 2.2 and assert exact full-pack nutrient intake until basis/physical pack established. Exact Özdilek [product](https://www.ozdilekteyim.com/market/yayla-haslanmis-kirmizi-fasulye-400-g) confirms220g drained; indexed retail price53TL marked changed 09Jun2026, NOT current.
